[06:00:21] So, first at a high level, I wanted to kind of discuss the actual AI maturity curve and what this looks like for SDRs. So, starting out, when I was using AI, I was just prompting back and forth with a chatbot.
[06:00:33] I do typical things, like I'd ask, can you help me actually draft this email, or kind of perfect this copy, or do this account research for this specific account I'm looking at.
[06:00:42] And what we've seen kind of progress over the last year is first the idea of co-pilots. And so, co-pilots are AI that can actually go out and carry out a true task.
[06:00:52] So, instead of talking back and forth, getting email copied, and then copying that into your Gmail, it's something now that we see, like, actually having the AI go and basically hook up to a Gmail MCP or an API and be able to actually send that email off for you.
[06:01:06] And really with Bot, what we believe is kind of the next frontier, is actually automating entire jobs in full. And so, going beyond just a task and actually doing an end-to-end workflow.
[06:01:18] And really with the staff function of actually combining a team of bots, you can see how kind of automating a job, but also combining that with different features of business, like talking to RevOps or talking to your AE and getting context on an account,
[06:01:31] and how bots can actually pull in that context for you and really help you do that full process.
[06:01:40] And so, just diving into what RockBot actually looks like, if you haven't seen it before. So, you have all your bots kind of here on the left-hand side. And they're doing different things, like we have a sales outbound bot here, we have our chief of staff,
[06:01:52] and you can message them just like you message your own teammates. And more interestingly, you can actually see them talk with each other.
[06:01:59] And I have my own RockBot instance set up, where really there's only one bot that I'm actually talking to. And so, for the most part, I've kind of offloaded all that context to my chief of staff actually talking with other bots and doing that all for me.
[06:02:13] And I think some important things also to emphasize here is that bots keep their own context at a per bot level and memory, so they can really improve as you work more with them, as you go, their memory gets better.
[06:02:26] And this is why I really emphasize you actually should be training your bots as well, depending on the task with kind of the relevant info. So, set up your Slack, set up your Gmail connector, and you can actually kind of start to train it on what your voice looks like.
[06:02:39] And so, specifically for Gmail, what I do is I've had it go into my Gmail and actually look at how do I actually talk to people? And specifically in my outbound messages, how do I actually talk to people in a way where they actually want to respond and they're interested in kind of seeing a demo for something full of people?
[06:02:55] And so, I'll really go into more of what that actually looks like, how I place weights on certain things to actually help Grokbot understand this. But I think a big thing I want to talk about also is the ability to share your bots.
[06:03:08] And I'll dive into this more in the next slide. But I think as SDRs, one of the most important things is being able to see what your teammates are actually doing and to kind of easily transfer those skills over to your other SDRs.
[06:03:21] And so, you can actually really scale those outputs and do it very quickly. I think the truth, at least in SpaceX, of what we've seen is that what's working for us today was not working three months ago.
[06:03:32] And three months before that, it was something entirely different. And so, the ability to really just move quickly and always be iterating is something that the ability to share bots is really going to help you with and help scale the entire SDR team.
[06:03:46] So, diving into this more, like why are we using Grokbot? I think to start, it's as easy and as intuitive as just iMessage or another general platform. You can just talk to your bots.
[06:03:55] And I think, more importantly, bots are always on. And so, you don't have to walk around with your laptop open anymore. You can actually close it. I can go on coffee walks and continue prompting for my phone.
[06:04:07] Or I can set it up on a long-running task and then trust that it will actually complete that task end-to-end and do the thing without me having to kind of object in between and guide it.
[06:04:17] It really is the confidence to go carry out and do things. And there's many ways you can actually improve that by recording your own screen and some other stuff that I'll show more in the demo.
[06:04:25] But again, it also uses the tools, just like you do. And so, you can think of your basic connectors of MCPs, of APIs, everything that you've already kind of set up.
[06:04:35] But beyond that, what's really important is it actually owns its own VM. And so, you can log into the browser. And for the parts of your SDR work that are a little more difficult or there's no real way to automate them, you can actually show bot in the browser.

[07:31:38] So first, what is Grok Bot?
[07:31:40] Grok Bot is a new product that lets you hand off real work
[07:31:43] to an AI coworker.
[07:31:46] The interface looks very familiar,
[07:31:48] just like a messenger app on your phone or a computer.
[07:31:53] But under the hood, it's built to keep working
[07:31:55] after you've sent your original message.
[07:31:58] So you aren't just chatting back and forth
[07:32:00] with quick, simple one-offs here and there.
[07:32:04] You're really able to delegate a real project to Grok Bot,
[07:32:08] and it can go off and take it from there.
[07:32:12] One of my favorite things about Grok Bot
[07:32:14] is how flexible it is to the way you work.
[07:32:17] So you can start with a single general bot
[07:32:19] to help you get set up quickly and easily,
[07:32:22] or create a team of specialists that
[07:32:24] coordinate around the task and work in parallel
[07:32:27] on certain sub-tasks.
[07:32:30] And however your team operates, Grok Bot
[07:32:32] can fit in and be very useful very quickly.
[07:32:37] So why Grok Bot?
[07:32:39] Why does it feel like interacting
[07:32:42] with a capable coworker rather than just another AI tool?
[07:32:47] For me, there are three big reasons.
[07:32:49] First, Grok Bot is always on.
[07:32:52] So by now, you may have heard that Grok Bots
[07:32:54] have their own computer.
[07:32:57] All that really means is that they
[07:33:00] can work without using yours, which
[07:33:02] is a little bit more powerful than you would think.
[07:33:06] So if you close your laptop or you
[07:33:07] are working on something else, your Grok Bots
[07:33:10] can still finish the task you assigned them
[07:33:12] without you having to check in constantly
[07:33:15] and steer them too much or slowing down your local machine.
[07:33:19] There are also routines, which are
[07:33:22] cron jobs or event-based triggers
[07:33:24] that you can launch your Grok Bots with
[07:33:27] and make them super reactive.
[07:33:30] This way, you don't have to be online at the exact moment.
[07:33:33] You want them to run or start working on the task.
[07:33:38] And these are simple, or these are super simple to set up.
[07:33:41] All you have to do is tell it, create a routine at this time
[07:33:44] or on this event, just like a normal conversation,
[07:33:49] and it will set it up for you.
[07:33:51] Second, it is really easy to use.
[07:33:54] The interface is familiar for a reason.
